<!-- page 1 -->
NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, November 9, 2021
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, November 9, 2021, in the Supervisors’
Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, Holbrook, Arizona. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive
session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any items as indicated on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S.
§ 38-431.03(A) (3).
  
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the Community to conduct this public meeting using a
hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. Board members will participate in the meeting, in-person in the Board of
Supervisors Chambers in Holbrook, or via Zoom. Limited seating will be available for the public.  
 
To Join Zoom Meeting now, click
here: https://us06web.zoom.us/j/86326812324?pwd=c0hYckRlbXBoNzNFcjdFZ0kxczhQQT09
 
Web Conference: 
Call In Number:1-253-215-8782 or 1-346-248-7799 (you may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 863 2681 2324
Passcode: 397114
 
If you would like to speak during Call to the Public please take the following actions:
Email Melissa Buckley at, melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov  1.
Identify the Board of Supervisors' meeting date, the agenda item # and title.2.
Name & Telephone Number 3.
Comments or questions should be sent by November 8, 2021, by 5:00 pm. for the November 9, 2021, Board meeting.4.
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s
office at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, except items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo County
website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: October 26, 2021 Regular Meeting  
 
b. Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 October 2021  
 
c. Sympathy Letter to Christopher Becenti (Navajo Nation) and Charmarie Thomas (Health

<!-- page 2 -->
c. Sympathy Letter to Christopher Becenti (Navajo Nation) and Charmarie Thomas (Health
District)
 
 
d. 12 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $7,330.39  
 
e. Fiscal Year 2022 Fund Transfers  
 
f. Rename an existing road in Aripine area from "Bonham Boulevard" to "Big Pasture Road"in
Section 33,Township 12 North, Range 18 East
 
 
g. Contracts signed by County Manager/Assistant County Manager pursuant to Board of
Supervisors Resolution: Cost Allocation Plan for fiscal year 2022-23 and Quadient Lease
Renewal Contract Number ADSPO16-169901 for renewal of lease agreement for mail machine
 
 
h. Resolution Number 33-2021 granting a right of way abandonment of a portion of "Cedar
Road" in Heber
 
 
i. Acceptance of Back Tax Land List and authorization to publish the notice of the same
scheduling the 2021 Back Tax Land On-Line Auction to begin January 17, 2022, at 9:00 a.m.
 
 
j. Appointment for Brandon Paul Martin, Antone William Morris, Michael Lapearl Montandon,
Laree Antionette Montandon, Dana Aileen Mead, Harry Durward Cheely, S D Ayers Fisk,
Cari Elizabeth Kay Lindquist, Michael Edward Lindquist and Jamie K. Shelburne as
Republican Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
 
 
k. 2022 Board of Supervisors Meeting Schedule  
 
3. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board wishes
to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Letter to HIDTA regarding Overdose Detection Mapping Application Program Agreement

<!-- page 3 -->
4. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: Contract
for Professional Services between Navajo County Flood Control District and Red Hawk
Engineering for profession service for Engineering Consultant Services
 
 
b. Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Taylor for Flood Control Project Funding  
 
5. SILVER CREEK COUNTY ROAD IMPROVEMENT DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
1. Silver Creek County Road Improvement District expenditure of an amount not to exceed
$25,000 for maintenance work on roads in the White Mountain Lakes area
 
 
6. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
 
a. Consideration of a Proclamation declaring November 2021 as "Native American Heritage
Month"
 
Presented By: Vice-Chairwoman Dawnafe Whitesinger
 
b. Presentation to our Legislative Delegation  
Presented By: Board of Supervisors 
 
7. BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  
 
a. 10:30 AM Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Notice of Change APN
208-06-022C; Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners. 
 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Cammy Darris
 
b. 10:45 AM Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Notice of Change APN
202-11-028B; Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners. 
 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Cammy Darris

<!-- page 4 -->
c. 11:00 AM Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Notice of Change APN
409-21-001B; Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners. 
 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Cammy Darris
 
d. 11:15 AM Discussion and action regarding Petition for Review of Notice of Change APN
309-29-013B; Swearing in of Assessor, Appraisers and Petitioners. 
 
Presented By: Melissa Buckley/Cammy Darris
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
9. ADJOURN  
 
10. FALL LEGISLATIVE LUNCHEON: Pursuant to ARS § 38-431.02 and ARS § 38-431
(3), notice is hereby given to the members of the Navajo County Board of Supervisors and
to the Public that a quorum of the Board of Supervisors may be present for a Legislative
Luncheon in the Chevelon Canyon Conference room Navajo County Government Complex
Public Works building where no official action will be taken.
 
 
Dated: November 5, 2021 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 9:15 a.m. By: MWB                         
Melissa W. Buckley - Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: Re-naming existing road in Aripine from Bonham Blvd to Big Pasture Rd - District 4
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Melanie Sleeper, GIS/E911 Addressing/ROW Analysis
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Rename an existing road in Aripine area from "Bonham Boulevard" to "Big Pasture Road"in
Section 33,Township 12 North, Range 18 East
Background:
Property owners on existing Bonham Road in Aripine have requested the re-naming of the road.
The road is in Section 33, Township 12 North, Range 18 East. (See attached maps) Map 6-16 and
Survey 2018-1134. Currently, this road serves 7 properties. All property owners whose property is
adjacent to or addressed off Bonham Blvd are in support of the road name change to Big Pasture
Road. Petitions from all property owners have been signed and notarized. The road is not County
maintained.  Public Works Engineering staff has evaluated the name change and supports the
request.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2022
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: NA
Fiscal Impact:
No fiscal impact is anticipated for this name change.

Background:
Cost Allocation Plan: This is for our cost allocation/indirect cost methodology.  We use this
document for budgeting the indirect transfers between funds.  Also, other departments use it as a
basis to allocate indirect costs to external organizations (mostly for grant purposes).  We should
have a cost allocation plan done every 2-3 years.  
Quadient Lease Renewal: We are utilizing the State Contract along with their terms and
conditions. This lease agreement is for the mail machine used by all Navajo County departments.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. h.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: Abandonment of Public Right-of-Way: Portion of Cedar Road - District 4
Submitted For: Bill Bess, County Engineer/Assistant Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Bryan Cook, Sr. Engineering Project Manager II
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Resolution Number 33-2021 granting a right of way abandonment of a portion of "Cedar Road"
in Heber
Background:
Application for abandonment of a portion of Cedar Road (ABAN:20-2) was requested by the
Buckskin Artist Community HOA to allow the adjacent property owners to limit or prevent access
to quads going across their private properties.  The abandonment will also allow the owner of
parcels 207-19-023A and 207-19-082A to more safely move his horses between his two
properties.  The HOA formally approved the the request at their local HOA Board
meeting on April 24, 2021. Public Works staff has vetted the application for abandonment
and determined that no parcels will be landlocked and that the ROW is not needed for public
benefit or access.  
Approval of this abandonment has stipulations that must be completed within a year. At its own
expense, the applicant will:  
Retain an appraiser, acceptable to Navajo County, to appraise the abandonment area; the
amount determined will be paid by either the applicant or divided between the adjacent
property owners
Retain professional services to create a legal description and quit claim deed for the portion
of “Cedar Road” that is being abandoned
Together, the quit claim deed, legal description, and appraised value will be used by the
Board of Supervisors at a subsequent meeting to consider approval of the sale of a portion
of public roadway by resolution pursuant to ARS § 28-6701, 6709, and 7204. 
If the requirements listed above are not met within one year, the ROW will once again be
considered as public property.
 
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2022

RESOLUTION NO._____-21 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS  
GRANTING A RIGHT-OF-WAY ABANDONMENT OF A PORTION OF  
“CEDAR ROAD” RECORDED IN PLAT BOOK 6 PAGE 22 
 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County is authorized by Section 28-6701 and 28-
6709 of the Arizona Revised Statutes to abandon portion of pubic roadways that are no longer necessary 
for public use; and 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors does hereby declare and determine that the 
following request for a right-of-way (ROW) abandonment for the portion of “Cedar Road” adjacent to 
Lot 55 as dedicate in Plat Book 6 page 22; was initiated and filed by the Buckskin Artists Community 
HOA, and that a Public meeting was duly held on the 11th day of November, 2021. 
WHEREAS, the portion of the ROW to be abandoned is the section of “Cedar Road” adjacent to 
Lot 55 of Plat Book 6 Page 22 is 25 feet in width and 331.49 feet in length, and; 
WHEREAS, the application has been vetted by Staff and determined that no parcels will be 
landlocked and that the ROW is not needed for public benefit, and; 
WHEREAS, the request is supported by the Buckskin Artist Community HOA being approved at 
their local HOA Board Meeting on April 24th, 2021 and; 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors, having heard public comment and after due consideration 
for those facts presented, find that the consideration of public safety, health, general welfare and 
convenience warrant the following action. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED that by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County; does 
hereby Grant a right-of-way abandonment as stated in this resolution with the following stipulation: 
o The applicant will, at their own expense, retain an appraiser, acceptable to Navajo 
County, to appraise the abandonment area and the amount determined will be paid by 
either the applicant divided between the adjacent property owners. 
o The applicant will, at their own expense, retain professional services to create a legal 
description and quit claim deed for the portion of “Cedar Road” that is being 
abandoned.

<!-- page 548 -->
o Together, the quit claim deed, legal description, and appraised value will be used by 
the Board of Supervisors to approve the sale of a portion of public roadway by 
resolution pursuant to ARS § 28-6701, 6709, and 7204. 
o Should the above requirements not be met within one (1) year time from the 
recording of this resolution due to inaction or otherwise loss of interest in proceeding. 
This resolution will be void and the right-of-way will be once again considered as 
public property. 
PASSED AND ADOPTED on May 25th, 2021 at Holbrook, Arizona. 
 
              ______________________________________ 
               Chairman, Daryl Seymore 
Attest: _____________________________ 
            Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board

Dear Property Owner: 
 
Per Navajo County Procedures for the Abandonment of County Local Highways, Streets, Avenues and Alleys and 
for the Extinguishing of Easements, as approved November 20, 1995, this letter is to inform you that there has 
been an application filed with this Department requesting a right-of-way abandonment. The subject property of 
the proposed abandonment is located within 300 feet of your property , directly affected, or you are a Utility 
provider. The following information identifies the subject applicant and location. 
 
APPLICANT:  Buckskin Artist Community HOA 
ADDRESS:      PO BOX 146     . 
CITY: Heber                   STATE: AZ.   
 
Intent of request: Applicant wishes to have the portion of Cedar Road adjacent to lot 55 as identified on Plat 6 -
22 also known as Buckskin Artist Draw Community West 
 
If you have any comments either in favor or in opposition to the above-described request, you may submit them 
up-to 2 weeks prior to hearing date for inclusion in the BOS Agenda documentation. 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
P.O. BOX 668 
HOLBROOK, AZ  86025 
 
You are welcome to attend the   BOARD OF SUPERVISORS HEARING on this request, which is scheduled 
for    November 11 at    9:00 a.m.  Please call the week prior to the hearing date to check if application is still on 
the agenda and for directions on how to attend. If it will be in the Board of Supervisors chambers at the Navajo 
County Governmental Complex in Holbrook, Arizona or as a Video Conference Call at which point direction on 
how to access will be made available by the Clerk of the Board of Supervisor. 
 
Should you have any questions or need further information, please call this office at (928) 524-4047. 
 
Sincerely, 
 
 
 
 
Bryan Cook, EIT 
Senior Engineering Project Manager II

REASON FOR REQUEST:
The Buckskin Artist Community HOA would like to have the ROW abandoned and turned over 
to the only property owner that abuts the ROW. The ROW has had no public use for years. No 
properties will e landlocked as a result of the abandonment. 
STAFF REVIEW:
Potential impact on parcel owners to the E and SE. Looks to be used as secondary access
to multiple properties.
Potential to prevent splitting of E property in the future
Plated subdivision road
Abandonment would only benefit 1 person.

Jim Albu
 
From: Bryan Cook [mailto:Bryan.Cook@navajocountyaz.gov] 
Sent: Friday, April 16, 2021 3:38 PM
To: Jim Albu
Cc: Bill Bess
Subject: RE: Buckskin Artists Community Abandonment Request
 
Jim,
 
Here are the questions that the management staff had on the application: Question 1  was from the
Public Works Director. Question 2 was by Planning and Zoning. This one is a critical question.
Questions 3 is to support the reasoning of the application.
 
1. Has this been approved the HOA or been addressed by an HOA meeting. Especially with all
the adjacent  property owners?  The Buckskin Artists Community HOA Board approved the
abandonment at their Board meeting on Aril 24, 2021.  The two adjacent property owners
(Shane Tunnel and Kelly Fletcher) have approved.
 
2. How will Parcel: 
207-19-023B have access. Additionally, does 207-19-008 use this as well? 
Parcel 207-19-023B has an access easement across parcel 207-19-023A as shown on the
attached deed (see page 4).  Both 207-19-023B and 207-19-008 are owned by the same
person and 207-19-008 has access from Artists Draw Road.
 
3. What are the benefits, what does approval allow to be accomplished?  Abandonment allows
the adjacent property owners to limit or prevent access to quads going across their
properties.  It will also allow Shane Tunnel (the owner of parcels 207-19-023A and 207-19-
082A) to more safely move his horses between his two properties.  There is no benefit to
public access to the properties adjacent to this right-of-way.

<!-- page 555 -->
With answer to these questions, we will include them in the Staff Packet, see if it resolves the
reviewers questions. If so, then we will cash the check and proceed to submit an agenda item for the
Abandonment and send notices out of the date that the BOS will make a determination on the
application.
 
 
Thank You,

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. j.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: REP PC Appointments
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Appointment for Brandon Paul Martin, Antone William Morris, Michael Lapearl Montandon,
Laree Antionette Montandon, Dana Aileen Mead, Harry Durward Cheely, S D Ayers Fisk, Cari
Elizabeth Kay Lindquist, Michael Edward Lindquist and Jamie K. Shelburne as Republican
Precinct Committeemen for their respective precincts
Background:
Appointment valid through Oct. 1, 2022. 16-822. Precinct committeemen; eligibility; vacancy;
duties; term B.  If the number of persons who file nominating petitions for an election to fill
precinct committeeman positions is less than or equal to the number of precinct committeeman
positions, the county board of supervisors may cancel the election for those positions not sooner
than seventy-five days before the election and appoint the person who filed the nominating
petition to fill the position. If no person has filed a nominating petition to fill a position, the
position is deemed vacant and shall be filled as otherwise provided by law. A precinct
committeeman who is appointed pursuant to this subsection after filing a nominating petition shall
be deemed an elected precinct committeeman. F. The term of office of a precinct committeeman is
two years and begins on October 1 after the primary election at which the precinct committeeman
was a candidate and continues until October 1 after the following primary election at which a
precinct committeeman is elected.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. k.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: 2022 BOS Schedule
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
2022 Board of Supervisors Meeting Schedule
Background:
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will meet on the second and fourth Tuesdays of every
month at 9:00 a.m., except for July, which will be the third Tuesday. November and December
remain one meeting a month due to the Holidays and will be held on the second Tuesday.

Mr. Jeff Beeson

9001 Edmonston Road, Suite 300

Re: ODMAP Overdose Detection Mapping Application Program Agreement  
 
Dear Mr. Beeson: 
 
I am the Chairman of the Navajo County Public Health District  (NCPHD). Recently, the N CPHD entered into an 
agreement with High Intensity Drug Trafficking Area (HIDTA) overdose mapping detection. 
 
However, it came to our attention that our Health District’s participation in this program could jeopardize grant funding 
from other Health District partners. In an effort to eliminate this possibility , the NCPHD will no  longer be able to 
participate in the overdose mapping program.  
 
Please consider this letter NCPHD official written notification of our intention to terminate the ag reement pursuant to 
the agreement.  
 
 
Very truly yours, 
 
 
 
 
Daryl Seymore 
Chairman 
Navajo County Public Health District

CONTRACT FOR PROFESSIONAL SERVICES 
 
 
AGREEMENT made as of October 11, 2021, between Navajo County Flood Control 
District ("District") and Red Hawk Engineering (“Consultant”). 
 
WHEREAS, District requires cer tain professional serv ices for Engineering 
Consultant Services within Navajo County (“Project”); and 
 
WHEREAS, Consultant represents that it has the necessary expertise to provide such 
services in accordance with the terms of this Agreement. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties agree as follows: 
 
1. Services.  Consultant shall perform the work described in the Scope of Work dated 
August 7, 2021, and those additional items set forth in the Consultant’s schedule and 
fee proposal dated August 7, 20 21, in a competent and prof essional manner to the 
satisfaction of District.  The Scope of Wo rk and the Consultant’s schedule and fee 
proposal are attached hereto and by this reference incorporat ed herein.  If any 
incorporated term is inconsistent with the Agreement, this Agreement shall control. 
 
2. Consultant’s Expertise.  Consultant warrants that it has the ability, authority, 
capacity and professional expertise to perfo rm this Agreement.  Consultant shall 
provide an adequate staff of experienced  personnel, capable of and devoted to the 
successful accomplishment of the work to be performed hereunder.  Consultant shall 
assign specific individuals to key positio ns.  Once assigned to work under this 
Agreement, key personnel shall not be removed or replaced without District’s prior 
written approval.  
 
3. Independent Contractor.  Consultant acknowledges that  it is an independent 
contractor.  Neither party is or shall purport to be an agent, employee, partner, joint 
venturer or associate of the other.  An employee or agen t of one party shall not be 
deemed an employer or agent  of the other party for any purpose.  Taxes or Social 
Security contributions will not be with held from any paymen t by District, and 
Consultant shall be solely responsible for such matters.

<!-- page 597 -->
Page -2- 
4. Subcontracts.  Consultant shall not enter into any subcontract with respect to any of 
the work to be performed hereunder without District’s prior written approval.  All 
subcontracts shall comply with applicable federal and state laws and regulations and 
shall impose on the subcontractor substantially the same obligations as are imposed 
on Consultant by this Agreement with respect to those matters covered by Sections 8, 
9, 10, 12, 15 and 18.  Consultant is  responsible for full performance of this 
Agreement regardless of whether subcontractors are used. 
 
5. Time for Completion.  Consultant’s services will be provided in a manner consistent 
with the degree of care and skill ordi narily exercised by me mbers of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances.  Consultant’s services 
shall be performed as expeditiously as is consistent with professional skill and care 
and the orderly progress of the Project, and in accord ance with an agreed upon 
schedule for the performance of the Services hereunder.  Time limits established by a 
schedule approved by District shall not, except for reasonable cause, be exceeded by 
District or Consultant.  The Schedule shall be adjusted, if necessary, as the Project 
proceeds.  The date for final performance shall be extended by the number of days 
that performance is delayed by governmental approval or review procedures or other 
causes beyond Consultant’s reasonable control, as jointly confirmed in writing by the 
parties’ representatives. 
 
6. Payment.  District shall pay to Consultant a not-to-exceed sum of Fifteen thousand 
dollars ($15,000.00) as payment in full for all services rendered by Consultant 
pursuant to this Agreement.  Payments shal l be made within 30 days of District’s 
receipt of Consultant’s monthly invoices .  Each invoice shal l detail the work 
performed during the billing period. 
 
7. Defects in Work.  District may reject any work product that fails to meet customary 
professional standards or Project specifi cations.  Consultant agrees to promptly 
remedy all such deficiencies.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
controversy or claim through informal nego tiation as set forth in Section 12.  No 
compensation shall be paid for any reject ed work until such issues have been 
resolved. 
 
8. Insurance Requirements.   
 
A. Consultant shall maintain in eff ect, at all times during the term of this 
Agreement, insurance adequate to protect District and its agents, 
representatives, officers, officials and employees against such losses as set 
forth below.  Consultant shall provide District with a current Certificate of 
Insurance or a certified copy of the insurance policy naming District as an 
additional insured (except for Errors and Omissions / Professional Liability 
coverage). 
 
 
B. The following types an d amounts of insurance are required as minimums:

<!-- page 598 -->
Page -3- 
Worker's Compensation    Statutory 
Professional Liability    $1,000,000 each occurrence 
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Bodily Injury  $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Consultant’s Protective Personal Property $1,000,000 each occurrence  
           and annual aggregate 
Automobile Bodily Injury and Property Damage $1,000,000 each 
occurrence 
       and annual aggregate 
Valuable Papers     $100,000 
 
Standard minimum deductibles are allowed.  Any deductibles are the 
responsibility of Consultant.  Consultant shall immediately inform District of 
any cancellation of insurance or any decrease in the amount of coverage at 
least 30 days before such action takes place.  A violation of this provision may 
be treated as a material breach by Dist rict.  Consultant shall notify District 
upon any termination of its regular prof essional liability coverage and shall 
obtain tail coverage for a minimum of five years from the termination date. 
 
C. Consultant acknowledges that  the amounts of insurance coverage stated above 
shall not limit its liability under this Agreement. 
 
9. Indemnity.  To the fullest extent allowable  by law, Consultant shall defend, 
indemnify and hold harmless District and its representatives, officers, officials and 
employees (the “Indemnitees”) from and against any and all claims, damages, losses 
and expenses (including but not limited to attorney fees, court costs, and the costs of 
appellate proceedings) relating to, arisi ng out of or resulti ng from Consultant’s 
negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions in the performance of this Agreement. 
Consultant’s duty to defend, hold harmless and indemnify the Indemnitees shall arise 
in connection with any claim, damage, loss or expense that is attributable to bodily 
injury, sickness, disease, death or injury to, impairment or destruction of property 
including loss of use resulting therefrom, caused in whole or in part by any negligent 
act, error, mistake or omission in the performance of this Agreement (including those 
by any person for whose negligent acts, errors, mistakes or omissions Consultant may 
be liable) to the extent of Consultant’s negligence or fault.  The amount and type of 
insurance coverage requirements set fort h above shall not lim it the scope of the 
indemnity in this paragraph. 
 
10. Records.  Consultant shall retain, and shall require each subcontractor to retain, all 
books, accounts, reports, files and other reco rds relating to this Agreement for a 
period of five years after completion of the work.  All such documents shall be 
subject to inspection and audit by Dist rict upon reasonable notice during normal 
business hours.  A legible co py of any or all such documents shall be produced by 
Consultant at the request and expense of District. 
 
11. Non-Assignment.  Consultant shall not assign any right or interest in this Agreement

<!-- page 599 -->
Page -4- 
without District’s prior written approval, nor shall Consultant delegate any duty 
hereunder without District’s prior written approval. 
 
12. Negotiation of Disputes.  The parties shall make a good faith effort to resolve any 
claim or controversy or claim through informal negotiation.  Notice of any claim or 
controversy shall be provided in writi ng, with supporting documentation, to the 
recipient designated in Section 21.  The recipient shall have seven calendar days to 
prepare and deliver a written response.  If the parties fail to resolve the disputes 
within a reasonable period of not less than ten days, either party may pursue available 
legal remedies. 
 
13. Suspension and Termination by District: 
 
A. Suspension.  District may, without cause, order Consultant in writing to suspend, 
delay or interrupt its performance in whole or part.  An adjustment shall be made 
to the completion date and for any increase in the cost of performance resulting 
from the suspension, delay or interruption.  No adjustment shall be made to the 
extent that performance is or would have been suspended, delayed or interrupted 
by another cause for which Consultant is responsible. 
 
B. Termination for Cause.   District may terminate th is Agreement for cause if 
Consultant refuses or fails to supply enough properly skilled workers to perform 
this Agreement; fails to make required payments to subcontractors; disregards 
laws, ordinances, rules, regulations or  orders of a public  authority having 
jurisdiction; or otherwise commits a material breach of  this Agreement.  When 
any of the foregoing causes exist, District shall give Consultant written notice and 
a ten-day opportunity to cure the default.  If the default remains uncured, District 
may (without prejudice to any other rights or remedies it may have) terminate this 
Agreement and finish the work or cause it to be finished.  If the unpaid balance of 
the Agreement exceeds the cost of fini shing the work (including any expenses 
incurred by District as a re sult of Consultant’s defa ult), Consultant shall be 
entitled to payment for its performance up to the amount of such excess.  If the 
cost of finishing the work exceeds the u npaid balance, Consultant shall pay the 
difference to District. 
 
C. Termination for Convenience.  District, by written notice to Consultant, may 
terminate this Agreement in whole or part when in the sole discretion of District it 
is in District’s best interests to do so.  Consultant sh all be paid for all material, 
equipment and services provided, as well as reasonable termination expenses and 
a reasonable allowance for profit and overhead, provided that such payments, 
exclusive of termination expenses, shal l not exceed the total amount payable 
pursuant to Section 6 less any paymen ts previously made to Consultant.  
Consultant shall not be entitled to profit and overhead on material, equipment and 
services which were not provided. 
 
D. Consultant’s Responsibilities.  Upon receipt of a termination notice, Consultant 
shall (a) promptly discontinue all servic es (unless the notice directs otherwise),

<!-- page 600 -->
Page -5- 
and (b) deliver or otherwise make available to District copies of all data, design 
calculations, drawings, specifications, reports, estimates, summaries and other 
information and materials developed or accumulated by Consultant in performing 
this Agreement. 
 
14. Termination by Consultant.  Consultant may terminate this Agreement upon written 
notice to District if performance is made impossible for a period of 30 consecutive 
calendar days for any of the following reasons through no act or fault of Consultant or 
its agents, employees or subcontractors: issuance of an order by a court or other 
public authority having jurisdiction; an act  of government, such as a declaration of 
national emergency; or a natural disaster or other Act of God.  Consultant may also 
terminate this Agreement upon written notice  and a ten-day opportunity to cure if 
District fails to make any payment within the time set forth in Section 6.  Consultant 
may also terminate this Ag reement upon written notice if  suspensions, delays or 
interruptions by District equal in the aggregate more than 100% of the total number of 
days scheduled for completion. 
 
15. Governing Law.   This Agreement shall  be governed by the law of the State  of 
Arizona.  Any suit arising out of this Agreement shall be brought in the state courts of 
Arizona (with venue in Navajo County) or the federal District of Arizona, but only 
after informal negotiation pursuant to Section 12. 
 
16. Incorporation of Applicable Laws.  Every provision of law required by statute or 
regulation to be in this Agreement will be read and enforced as though included 
herein.  Each party shall promptly notify the other upon discovery that any such 
provision has been omitted. 
 
17. Non-Collusion.  The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this 
reference. 
 
18. One-Year Limitation on Actions.  No action shall be maintained by Consultant on 
any claim based upon or aris ing out of this Agreement unless such action is 
commenced within one year after District’s final payment hereunder. 
 
19. Term.  This Contract for Professional Service is valid for one (1) year from the date 
the agreement was made between the two parties. Upon mutual agreement between 
the parties, this contract may be renewed up to four (4) additional one-year terms. 
 
20. Entire Agreement.  This Agreement and those documents incorporated by reference 
represent the entire understanding between  the parties.  No amendment shall be 
effective unless executed by both parties with the same formality as this Agreement.  
Provided, however, that the District’s project manager is authorized to modify the 
Scope of Work, in writing, with the concurrence of Consultant so long as the project 
is not changed substantially or significant additional compensation is not required.

be inva
lid or unenforceable shall not affect the validity or enforceability of any other

s.  All notices, invoices and payments shall be in writing and may be given by

Board of Supervisors Regular 4. a. 1. b.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement with Town of Taylor for Flood Control Project
Funding - District 3
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement with the Town of Taylor for Flood Control Project Funding
Background:
The Town of Taylor would like to prevent or limit flooding in the area downstream from where
Pinedale Wash attributes the Silver Creek in Taylor.  The Town intends to undertake flood control
measures which include installing two detention basins and other pre-disaster mitigation (PDM)
features in the Pinedale Wash area.  Various properties downstream from these PDM features will
eventually be removed from the FEMA 100-year flood map after the Town completes the project
and submits for a Letter of Map Revision.
The Town has requested financial assistance from the Navajo County Flood Control District in an
amount not to exceed $50,000.  Public Works has reviewed the request, finds it consistent with
the purpose of the flood control district, and supports the project.  Funding is authorized in the
Navajo County Flood Control District FY 2021-22 budget. 
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2021-22
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $50,000
Fiscal Impact:
Funding is authorized in the FY 2021-22 Navajo County Flood Control District budget.

1 
 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR 
FLOOD CONTROL PROJECT FUNDING 
 
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT is entered into as of November 9, 2021 
("Effective Date") by and between the Navajo County Flood Control District, a special taxing 
district organized pursuant to Title 48, Chapter 21 of the Arizona Revised Statutes ("the 
District"), and the Town of Taylor, an Arizona municipal corporation ("the Town"). 
 
1. Premises. 
 
1.1 The parties are authorized to enter into intergovernmental agreements for joint or 
cooperative action pursuant to A.RS. § 11-952. 
 
1.2 The Town would like to prevent or limit flooding in the area downstream from 
where Pinedale Wash attrib utes  the Silver Creek in Taylor. 
 
1.3 The Town intends to undertake flood -control measures which include  installing 
two detention basins and other pre -disaster mitigation (PDM) features in the 
Pinedale Wash area. Various properties downstream from these PDM features will 
eventually be removed from the FEMA 100 -year flood map after the Town 
completes the project and submits for a Letter of Map Revision.  
  
1.4 The District is authorized by A.R.S. § 48-3603(C)(3) to contract and join with a 
municipality such as the Town "in acquiring, constructing, maintaining and 
operating flood control works, and regulating floodplains." 
 
1.5 The Board of Directors of the District has determined that it is an appropriate use 
of District funds to financially assist the Town with the Project and has budgeted 
$50,000 for this purpose, subject to the terms and conditions of this Agreement. 
 
2. The District's Obligations. 
 
2.1 The District shall reimburse the Town for eligible costs of the Project, up to a 
maximum reimbursement of $50,000. Eligible costs are the actual expenses of 
design, engineering and other contractual services directly associated with the 
Project, property acquisition directly associated with the Project, and construction 
and program management. The Town's administrative and other internal expenses 
are not eligible costs, nor are the expenses of improvements not directly related to 
flood control (such as landscaping, roads, or recreational facilities). The District 
shall be solely responsible for determining whether an expense is an eligible cost. 
 
2.1.1  It is understood and agreed by the District that the Town's share of eligible 
costs may be paid from other funding sources which may be available to the 
Town, such as federal or state grants. The availability of other funding 
sources shall not reduce the District's share of eligible   costs as provided in 
Section 2.1.

<!-- page 608 -->
2 
 
 
2.1.2 Payment shall be made by the District within 30 days after receipt of each 
reimbursement request. Reimbursement requests shall be in writing, shall 
specify the costs in sufficient detail to enable the District to determine 
whether they are eligible costs, and shall include the certification of the 
Town Manager that the costs are eligible costs. Reimbursement requests 
shall not be submitted more frequently than monthly. Reimbursement 
requests shall only be made for completed work and will include copies of 
original invoice docum entatio n from the consultant  and/or contractor  to the 
Town. 
 
2.1.3 Throughout the Project, a reasonable balance shall be maintained between 
the Town's contributions, which include the other funding sources as 
described in Section 2.1.l, and the District's contributions. If the District 
believes that a reasonable balance is not being maintained, it shall notify 
the Town in writing and may suspend payment of reimbursement requests 
until the issue has been resolved.  (Not sure we need this section, but can 
keep it in if you want?)  
 
2.1.4 Upon reasonable notice of not less than 48 hours, the District or its  
representatives may inspect and audit all books, papers and other records 
pertaining to  the Project and the Town's obligations under this 
Agreement. 
 
 
 
3. The Towns Obligations. 
 
3.1 The Town shall be solely responsible for the design, procurement, permitting    and 
construction of the Project. The Town understands and agrees that the District's sole 
participation and obligation with respect to the Project shall be that of a  funding 
source as described in Section 2.1 . The Project is not a District project, and the 
District is neither a partner nor a joint venturer with the Town in connection with the 
Project. The Town shall not represent to any consultant,  contractor, or any other 
person that the District will have any responsibility or liability in connection with the 
Project except as set forth in this Agreement. 
 
3.1.1 As a condition  of the funding  to be provided  by the District,  the Town shall 
ensure that all flood-control  measures of the Project  meet all federal,  state and 
local requirements. 
 
3.2 The Town  covenants  that it will diligently  pursue the completion  of the Project without  
undue delay or interruption. 
 
3.3 Upon completion  of the Project, the To wn shall be solely  responsible for all  
maintenance,  repairs and operating  expenses.  The Town shall maintain in force

<!-- page 609 -->
3 
 
comprehensive  liability insurance  adequate to insure against  all risks associated  with 
the Project facilities. 
 
4. Indemnification. 
 
To the fullest extent permissible by law, the Town shall  indem nify, defend and hold harmless  
the District and its officers,  employees  and agents from  and against any and all claims , actions,  
suits, liabilities,  damages,  losses and expenses arising out of the design, procurement,  
permitting  and construction  of the Project or the subsequent  maintenance,  repair or operation 
of the Project facilities. 
 
5. General. 
  
5.1 This Agreement shall be effective as of the Effective Date and shall remain in effect 
until the Project has been substantially completed. 
 
5.2 Requests for reimbursement, payments and other communications relating to this  
Agreement shall be sent to the intended recipient at the following address.  Either party 
may specify a different address by written notice to the other party: 
 
If to the District:     If to the Town: 
William R. Bess, P.E., CFM   Gus Lundberg 
Navajo County Flood Control   Town Manager of Taylor 
P.O. Box 668 Holbrook, AZ 86025  P.O. Box 158 Taylor, AZ 85939 
Phone (928) 524-4106    (928) 536-7366 
William.Bess@navajocountyaz.gov  gus@tayloraz.org  
 
5.3 In the event of any dispute arising out of this Agreement, the parties agree to make a 
good faith attempt to informally  resolve or mediate the dispute before submitting  the 
matter to arbitration pursuant to the provisions of A.R.S. § 12-1518. 
 
5.4 Each party warrants  that this Agreement  has been  duly authorized  by its governing  body, 
that the officials executing this Agreement on its behalf have been duly authorized to 
do so, and that when so executed this Agreement shall constitute a valid and binding 
obligation of the parties. 
 
5.5 The provisions  of A.R.S. § 38-511  are incorporated  herein by this reference.  
 
5.6 This Agreement constitutes the entire understanding between the parties with respect 
to its subject matter, superseding any prior or contemporaneous agreements, whether 
oral or written, concerning the same subject matter. This Agreem ent may be amended  
only by a written document  which expressly  refers to  it and is executed with the same 
formality by both parties. 
 
5.7 This Agreem ent shall be recorded with the Navajo County Recorder pursuant to A.RS. 
§11-952.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: Silver Creek County Road Improvement District Road Maintenance Activity -
District 3
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Board of Supervisors
Motion before the Board:
Silver Creek County Road Improvement District expenditure of an amount not to exceed $25,000
for maintenance work on roads in the White Mountain Lakes area
Background:
The Silver Creek County Road Improvement District (Silver Creek CRID) is a special district
formed to maintain roads in the White Mountain Lakes area of Navajo County.  Funding for the
road district maintenance comes from property owners in the area by fees collected through the
Navajo County property taxation system. The Silver Creek CRID has an agreement with the
neighboring White Mountain Lake Road Improvement District #2 to provide periodic
road maintenance work.  
The districts have determined that material needs to be added to the surface of
several residential gravel roads.  The work includes purchasing, placing,
compacting, and grading approximately 45 loads of aggregate base material. 
Funding for the maintenance work is authorized in the Silver Creek CRID budget. 
Public Works supports the maintenance plan as described above.  
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 22
Budgeted Y/N: N/A
Amount Requested: N/A
Fiscal Impact:
Funding in an amount not to exceed $25,000 is available in the Silver Creek CRID budget,
comprised of fees paid by the residents of the district.
Form Review

Board of Supervisors Regular 6. a.        
Meeting Date: 11/09/2021  
Title: Native American Heritage Month
Submitted By: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Vice-Chairwoman Dawnafe
Whitesinger
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of a Proclamation declaring November 2021 as "Native American Heritage Month"
Background:
On August 3, 1990, President of the United States George H.W. Bush declared the month of
November as National American Indian Heritage Month, thereafter commonly referred to as
Native American Heritage Month.
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval
Attachments
Proclamation 
Form Review
Form Started By: Donna Hurstrom Started On: 11/26/2018 07:35 AM
Final Approval Date: 11/03/2021

<!-- page 614 -->
Navajo County 
Proclamation 
 
NOVEMBER 2021 
“NATIVE AMERICAN HERITAGE MONTH” 
 
WHEREAS, Native American Heritage Month is celebrated every year to recognize Native 
American cultures, contributions, history, traditions, art, and land; and 
 
WHEREAS, the month of November is a time to celebrate the rich and diverse cultures, to 
raise awareness about our shared history, and to acknowledge the important contributions of 
our Native Heritage; and  
 
WHEREAS, the history and culture of Navajo County have b een significantly influenced 
by Native Americans, and by its many surrounding Native American tribes whose customs 
and traditions are respected and celebrated as part of a rich legacy throughout our area; and 
 
WHEREAS, Navajo County was built upon Native A merican lands and our county has 
greatly benefited since its founding from Native American contributions; and 
 
WHEREAS, we honor all tribes across the nation, including our regions Hopi Nation, 
Navajo Nation and White Mountain Apache Tribe; and  
 
WHEREAS, the Library of Congress, National Archives and Records Administratio n, 
National Endowment for the Humanities, National Gallery of Art, National Park Service, 
Smithsonian Institution and Unites States Holocaust Memorial Museum join in paying 
tribute to the rich ancestry and traditions of Native Americans; and  
 
WHEREAS, in celebration of Native American Heritage Month, Navajo County wishes to 
encourage everyone to join in recognition of the accomplishments and contributions the 
Native American community has made to our society and salute all the local organizatio ns 
who work with and in support of the Native American community. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, do hereby proclaim 
November 2021 as “NATIVE AMERICAN HERITAGE MONTH.” 
 
Done in concurrence with the Navajo County Boar d of Supervisors Meeting this ____ day 
of November 2021. 
 
       _______________________________ 
       Daryl Seymore, Chairman
